Each item has a stable `id`, `kind`, nullable `task`, finding data, and `action: {command, argv}`. Text output includes the same findings and command. A ready or rework task without a brief gets `brief_missing` instead of `ready` or `rework_ready`; `brief set` clears it. A `rework_ready` item for a task automation sent back for a failed gate, unchanged and untaken since, also has `gate` and `restore`, the `gates retry ID --from-rework` command that undoes the rework once the cause is fixed, and `hold_until` while automatic refill holds it (`gates.rework_hold_min`). When the PR is not open, or its head or the local branch moved, `restore` is left out and `restore_blocked` says why. Kinds include `gate_failed`, `ci_stalled`, `review_failed`, `review_body_stuck`, `review_held`, `review_exit_missing`, `review_churn`, `rework_ready`, `ready`, `dead_claim`, `decision`, `accepted_unmerged`, `revuto_failed`, `codeql_alert`, `head_changed`, `main_ci_failed`, `main_ci_flaky`, `message`, `stall`, `decision_answer`, `owner_comment`, `governor_deferred`, `governor_stopped`, `github_pending` and `github_error`. `governor_deferred` (id `governor_deferred:TASK:UNIT`) and `governor_stopped` carry the unit kind, its reason and its times, with `status` as their action; they clear on their own ([Host resource governor](#host-resource-governor)). Open decisions carry their question and options. A technical decision is answered by the orchestrator under the existing answer authority; an owner-only decision (see the decision rules above: an `escalation`, an `owner_required` reason, a spend, credential or setting kind the owner has not lifted, or a technical decision the owner unmarked) needs the owner's supplied choice. A decision's action argv ends in `<answer>` when the orchestrator answers it and `<owner answer>` when only the owner does. Answered decisions retain their choice, note, author and blocked tasks until acknowledged or expired from the view under the closed-task rule below. Owner task and decision comments retain their text and target until acknowledged; a later comment creates a new item.

`gate_failed` items report the latest audited failure for each software gate (`tests`, `clean`, `sources`, `ci`) at the current head and revision of an unmerged submitted or accepted task. They include the gate name, summary, ref, audited `confirmed_failure` flag and any `test_failure` names and output tail. Confirmed code failures resolve through rework carrying the failure summary and its test names. A change the worker must fix (`worker_failure`) offers rework with the gate summary. Pending checks, observation failures and infrastructure failures offer `check GATE ID`; infrastructure findings also retain their timeout metadata, and a failure the base shares carries its `main_failure: {base, names}`. Stalled CI is not a `gate_failed` item: it is a `ci_stalled` item addressed to the orchestrator with the gate summary and `since`, offering `check ci ID`. A missing or uninstalled pinned command is not a `gate_failed` item: it is a `gate_config` item addressed to the orchestrator, naming `gates.KEY` and offering `project set` with the pinned command, and `gates retry` follows once it is set. A hotfix whose changed host-only tests only skipped gets the same item naming `tests.host_env` and offering `project set --tests-host-env <JSON>`. A later pass, rework, a new head or revision, or a confirmed merge clears the finding. Failed gates cannot be acknowledged away. On an open mergeable accepted PR, a stale or unconfirmed failed required software gate offers its check command instead of `merge --accepted`; confirmed code failures offer rework instead; after that rerun passes, the inbox advances to the next blocker or merge. Review blockers still require rework.

Review findings use the latest eligible failed evidence at the current head and revision. `review_failed.findings` contains the last `Review (Tower Crane` issue comment whose header names that SHA, read from all pages of the linked PR's comments. `finding_count` uses the finding lines or declared count and is null when an unposted summary has no count. `findings_source` names `comment` or `evidence`. A failed linked-comment fetch leaves `findings_complete: false`, the evidence summary and an explicit `github_error`. Unposted reviews without a matching comment retain their full evidence summary. Rework items include the engine reason and readiness blockers. Process observations use the shared PID, host and start identity detector; unknown processes stay live. Accepted PR items include GitHub state, mergeability and the refusal reason. A PR already merged at its accepted head remains an `accepted_unmerged` item with `confirmation: true` and `merge --accepted` until the audited local merge receipt exists. Its obsolete gate and review findings are replaced by that confirmation action. Reading the inbox does not record a merge, and a remotely merged different head still requires rework. Submitted PRs include uncapped, nonignored revuto failures and its inline comments at that head, and open CodeQL alerts from `refs/pull/N/head` and `refs/pull/N/merge`. The PR API resolves the current head, base and synthetic merge commit; both merge parents must match the submitted head and current base before merge-ref alerts count. Alert instances must match the resolved ref and commit. Stale merge parents produce an explicit GitHub error. Duplicate alerts across refs appear once. List endpoints paginate. GitHub errors are explicit findings, never empty success. A moved PR head returns to rework so a worker verifies and resubmits it through the existing claim ownership path.

`review_held` surfaces while automation holds review dispatch for a submitted task after a reviewer exited without a verdict at its head and revision. It carries the `review held` event's fields, with a default `reason` when the reviewer left none, and offers `accept ID`, which dispatches the next reviewer. A new head, revision, revuto thread change or review evidence clears it.

`review_exit_missing` shows each `review exit missing` event until `inbox --ack ITEM` acknowledges it.

`review_churn` surfaces when an active task has at least `review.churn_after` eligible failed reviews under its unchanged acceptance scope. It includes `count`, `churn_after`, the current task `revision`, `reviews` in evidence order and the task's full recorded `spend` so far. Each review keeps its SHA, revision, reviewer, timestamp, ref and one-line `findings`. Counts span heads and consecutive one-step revision increments recorded by `rework`; another revision bump, including an acceptance change, resets the count without deleting evidence. Stack refreshes preserve that reset even when their `previous_revision` points to a rejected review before the acceptance change: that field carries review context, not the actual prior task revision. Passing, waived, self and unspawned review evidence do not add to the count. Accepted and cancelled tasks have no churn inbox item. Rework and claiming still proceed unless the repeated-area gate below requires a scope ruling; the churn item then names that hold. The item asks the orchestrator to bound scope, split the task or change tier; its command opens `task show` for that decision.

History compares explicit one-based acceptance references (`Acceptance item 1`, `Acceptance line #1`, `AC 1`) and file:line finding locations in evidence summaries. `acceptance_lines` and `finding_locations` keep those references; `new_acceptance_lines` and `new_finding_locations` show references absent from earlier failed reviews. `scope_change` is `first`, `same_acceptance_lines`, `added_acceptance_lines`, `same_finding_locations`, `added_finding_locations` or `unknown`. Acceptance comparisons take precedence when both successive reviews name acceptance lines. Locations are hints about added cases, not proof that scope changed; free text without comparable references remains `unknown`. The inbox does not infer acceptance coverage from prose or fetch historical comments.

`status` text lists the churn count for every task; JSON adds `review_churn: [{id, revision, count}]`. The board shows the same count on rows for active agents and submitted work, ready and blocked rows and each task sheet, including zero after a reset. The Markdown sketch lists counts in its Review churn table. Counts are derived from evidence and rework events; no counter is stored in `tasks.json`.

The host follower's `wait --inbox` refresh reads the newest 30 workflow runs and the current head of the project base, alongside its per-PR reads. Foreground inbox reads use cached GitHub data and recorded base CI observations. An unfiltered orchestrator `wait --follow` also refreshes main CI without `--inbox`. These reads reuse `main-ci-cache.json` for a minute; the active-workflow list is reread hourly while CI is red. For each workflow, the newest completed `schedule` or `workflow_dispatch` run decides the recorded red status of its full runs, and apart from them the newest completed `push` run decides its push runs: main pushes run a thin check, so a green push does not clear a red daily run. A cancelled push stays red while a newer push is pending. Superseded cancelled schedule or manual runs are skipped. Reruns keep the previous verdict until they conclude, and workflows outside the listing keep their recorded verdict. Inactive workflows are ignored. Conclusions other than `success`, `neutral` or `skipped` are red for this status view.

A `main ci` event records `{ base, head, read_at, red, push }`. `red` holds the deciding red runs; `push` holds the newest completed push run of each observed active workflow, including green runs. Run records contain `{ workflow, workflow_id, path, id, attempt, number, event, sha, conclusion, url, jobs? }`. Red push runs and other red runs include failing jobs and parsed test names: `{ id, name, conclusion, url, tests: [{ name, error }], log_error? }`. Unchanged observations write nothing; observations older than the last recorded one write nothing. Events belong to their base, so changing the base starts clean.

Red push runs at each head share one `main_ci_failed` item, id `main_ci_failed:project:HEAD_SHA`, with `runs`, combined `jobs`, test names and the first run under `run`. An observer raises its `inbox item` notification once per head, including across reruns. A later green push clears the failed workflow; older-head push failures keep their head item while a newer push is pending or does not trigger the failed workflow. Red schedule and manual runs also keep one item per workflow, id `main_ci_failed:project:WORKFLOW_ID`. The action opens `Fix BASE CI: WORKFLOW (workflow WORKFLOW_ID), red since run ID`, or shows the existing open fix task. A test failing again after a green run creates a `main_ci_flaky` item with `test`, `run` and `earlier_runs`, pointing to an existing or new flaky-test task. These CI items cannot be acknowledged. `status` prints recorded red workflows and their jobs and tests without reading GitHub. A failed inbox CI read is `github_error:project:main-ci`.

For a project with a GitHub repository, `ask --setting publish` always reads the base's runs, active workflows and current head afresh, independently of the inbox cache and optional publish policy. A workflow with completed `schedule` or `workflow_dispatch` runs on the base, its full runs, is judged by the newest of them that was not cancelled: it must be green (`success`, `neutral` or `skipped`) and at or after the base head this read finds, by GitHub's compare of the two (`identical` or `ahead`). The release ships that head, so a full run that predates it refuses with the run, its commit and the command that starts one (`gh workflow run FILE --ref BASE -R REPO`); a newer red or failed full run refuses too, and push runs of that workflow do not count. The read lists the newest 100 completed runs of each full event on the base and, for an active workflow with no deciding run there, that workflow's own 20 newest of each. Any other active workflow keeps the push rule: failed, cancelled, timed-out or unknown push runs refuse publication and name the run; a pending newer push cannot replace that completed push verdict. Disabled workflows do not block releases. For each active workflow absent from the newest 100 completed base-push runs, the check queries that workflow's latest completed push on the base directly. This covers managed workflows and older runs beyond the shared listing. Main push CI means observed push runs on the configured base: tag-only, other-branch and PR-only histories do not count. A workflow with no completed full or base-push history has no verdict to gate; the publication approval flow remains available. A recorded completed push attempt still decides while its run is being rerun and temporarily absent from GitHub's completed listing; a newer completed run or attempt replaces it. Unreadable GitHub responses refuse with the reason. An existing owner approval is left unconsumed on refusal, and an empty publish policy does not bypass a red verdict. Projects without a recorded repository retain the approval flow. The publish policy's `full_suite` condition does not count a crucial or mapped head run as the full suite.

The merge gate applies the same check before merging release PRs, including stack members and asynchronous merges: the full run must be at or after the base head the release PR merges onto. Release detection uses a task or PR title beginning with `Release` and containing a version, or changed `version` values in `package.json`, `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` or `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`. Unreadable changed manifest patches refuse the merge. Already merged PRs can still be confirmed; repair PRs can merge while main CI is red.

Foreground inbox snapshots never launch GitHub subprocesses. The host follower's `wait --inbox` observation refreshes PR observations, CodeQL, linked review comments, rework head checks and base CI concurrently across tasks, with the normal 60 s timeout per GitHub subprocess. Responses, including actual failures, use a 60000 ms refresh interval and are stored in `inbox-github-cache.json` as `{scope, started_at, read_at, main_error, entries: {HASH: {at, tasks, result}}}`. `scope` hashes the repository, base and GitHub host; each entry hashes the command, arguments and relevant task ids, statuses, PRs, SHAs and revisions. Invalid or future timestamps are not reused. Valid observations for unchanged task versions remain visible until replacement, even after one minute, so a slow refresh cannot hide findings. Overdue observations add `github_pending` with `stale: true` and a reminder to refresh; cold reads still return only the unread-data summary. The follower interval uses `started_at`, so late responses do not defer the next minute's refresh. Each refresh prunes responses for closed tasks and obsolete task versions before writing. Missing observations produce one `github_pending:project` item with `tasks`, `count`, `last_refresh` and a summary, rather than a GitHub error for each unread endpoint. Failed cached responses still produce explicit `github_error` findings, and failed linked review reads retain the evidence summary and incomplete marker. The pending item offers an `inbox` re-read while the last completed refresh is less than one minute old, including stale entries between healthy follower passes. A missing, overdue, invalid or future last-refresh timestamp offers `wait --inbox --observe --timeout 1` for one bounded observation. Neither action starts a persistent host, runs software reactions or dispatches workers. A missing rework head observation leaves its restoration blocked until refresh. Foreground base CI findings use recorded events and the last background read error. The pending summary checks the base cache or failed-read receipt independently of PR data, including when no PR is active. This cache is only for inbox observations; gates, restoration and merges outside an inbox snapshot still query GitHub directly.

Owner comment ids are `owner_comment:TASK:EVENT`, with `project` for a decision comment. Each historical owner comment without an event id uses `legacy-HASH-OCCURRENCE` as its event identity, hashing the complete event record and counting identical records in log order. Repeated identical comments remain distinct, ids survive appending events, and history is not rewritten. The item's `event` and acknowledgement action use the same identity. Old task-only acknowledgements do not suppress individual comments; acknowledge their current ids.

`inbox --ack ITEM` emits `inbox ack` with `{item}` for a handled message, stall, decision answer, owner comment or missing review exit. Repeat `--ack ITEM` to acknowledge several current items in one locked write and one event with `{items, count}`. Duplicate ids count once; any unknown, already acknowledged or nonacknowledgeable id refuses the entire batch without a write. A single `--ack` retains its `{item}` receipt and JSON `{acknowledged: ITEM}`; a batch returns `{acknowledged: [ITEM, ...], count}`. Both receipt forms suppress the listed items.

`inbox --ack-before TIME [--kind KIND]` acknowledges all current acknowledgeable items at or before the timestamp in one locked write and one `inbox ack` event with `{items, count, cutoff, kind?}`. `cutoff` is normalized to UTC. The JSON response is `{acknowledged: [ITEM, ...], count, cutoff}`. A batch with no matches still records one event with count zero. `--kind` accepts `message`, `stall`, `decision_answer`, `owner_comment` or `review_exit_missing` and requires `--ack-before`; `--ack` and `--ack-before` cannot be combined. These items expose `at`: the event timestamp for messages, comments, missing review exits and recorded stalls, the lease expiry for a derived stall, and `answered_at` for decision answers. Items newer than the cutoff remain visible. Acknowledgement cannot dismiss task or GitHub findings.

Messages older than 24 h disappear from the unacknowledged view when their task is cancelled or accepted with a recorded merge. Decision answers older than 24 h disappear only when they block at least one task and every blocked task meets that condition. Accepted tasks without a merge receipt, active or unknown tasks, project messages and answers without blocked tasks stay visible until acknowledged. Owner comments do not expire. Expiration changes only the view: it emits no acknowledgement and leaves the history intact.

`wait --inbox` refreshes at startup and once per minute, bounding GitHub requests independently of local hook traffic. It emits `inbox item` for new or changed findings and `inbox snapshot` with item fingerprints to deduplicate concurrent followers. A finding that clears and later returns wakes again. Snapshot and acknowledgement receipts are quiet bookkeeping. T98's plugin watch and orchestrator Stop hooks enable this refresh and direct the session to `inbox`.

Inbox reads, acknowledgements, observation and batch actions require a verified orchestrator or owner role; a verified worker identity alone grants no access. Batch actions preserve the authority table and existing checks. `spawn --ready` selects ready todo and rework tasks without live workers and dispatches serially within the worker limit, rechecking under the spawn lock. `release --dead` rechecks each claim under the release lock and never releases an unknown process. The worker broker rejects this unscoped batch before executing it; a worker can still release its own named task with `release ID --reason R`. `rework --from-review ID` refuses failed reviews whose findings are all PR-body false claims, with correction instructions; for other failures it retrieves the full matching review body outside the state lock and copies it, including every finding and the comment link, into the rework reason and brief. A linked comment that is unavailable or does not match the submitted SHA refuses without a state change. The command rechecks the head, revision, repository, PR and exact failed review under the rework lock after fetching; a newer verdict or changed task refuses. `merge --accepted` holds the existing queue executor and runs current-base checks and pinned stack merges; refused and pending entries remain for the next invocation while independent mergeable entries proceed. Automatic queue reactions still wait for unknown GitHub mergeability. Already merged PRs at the accepted head are confirmed without current gate evidence. Linked members still require every lower head to pass.

`mcp` serves newline-delimited JSON-RPC on stdio with `inbox`, `spawn_ready`, `merge_accepted`, `rework_from_review` and `release_dead` tools. Initialization, ping and tool discovery do not read or require project state, including outside a Git repository. Each tool call resolves the bound project location and verifies the caller role; missing-project and authorization errors return MCP tool errors without closing the connection. It validates tool arguments and offers no shell, identity or state overrides. Spawned Claude and Codex orchestrators receive this server in their generated homes; workers do not. The plugin ships the server registration and five matching command documents. No new authority class or owner grant is introduced.

## Repeated review failures

Two independent failed reviews with blocking findings in the same file area block claims, readiness and worker dispatch, including dry runs and explicit worker rungs. The area is a normalized finding file path: line numbers do not matter, `./` is removed and backslashes become slashes. A finding whose location is not a file path, such as `PR body`, adds no area. Failures accumulate across rework and requirement revisions. Failed verdicts at the same SHA and revision count once per area, whichever reviewers recorded them; multiple findings in one file in one review also count once. Passing reviews, software gate failures and untrusted review evidence do not add failures. A summary without a finding path supplies no area; use `rework --from-review` to retain the complete blocking findings.

The inbox shows `review_redesign` with `areas`, `reason` and a scope-ruling command, and holds the task out of `rework_ready`. The orchestrator or owner clears the hold by either narrowing acceptance to a closed list with `task update ID --acceptance A ... --closed-acceptance`, or recording a design change with `task note ID TEXT --kind scope-ruling`. Closed acceptance requires an actual acceptance change. Ordinary notes, acceptance changes without that declaration, brief edits and title changes do not clear the hold.

These actions audit the operational `task.scope_ruling` setting. Their task events carry `review_after`, the evidence-array length when the ruling is written; a closed-list update also carries `closed_acceptance: true`, and a design note carries `kind: "scope-ruling"`. Ruling events also record the task revision. Only failures recorded after that cutoff at that revision or later can trigger the next hold; late verdicts from older revisions cannot re-block the new design. The original evidence and rework notes stay in the audit history.
